1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:

   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

6. UPDATE experiments/2026-04-text-001-gemma3-adapter/experiment.yaml: add a reflection: block AND flip status: complete → status: closed.
   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: refuted
     surprises: |
       n=4 TIE at step 2000 turned into n=20 FAIL (30% win rate). Rule-based instructions made the adapter genuinely WORSE than prompting, not tied. Small-n evaluation was hiding a clear negative result.
     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |

     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |

     next_experiment: none
     rolls_up_to_project: true

Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. 2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

   reflection:

  "meta_commentary": "This is a tightly-scoped MVP finish: three new files (hf_push.py, close-experiment.sh), two existing bash scripts getting idempotent tag helpers + arg parsing, a PROCESS.md doc update, and two yaml backfills \u2014 all in one commit, no push, no tests beyond lint/dry-run.\n\nExecution gotchas:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 is a real trap.** The plan's Step 9.2 says `git rm -r experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke*`, but those dirs are untracked \u2014 their only content (`results/a.json`) is gitignored. Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.         Idea:
Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

6. UPDATE experiments/2026-04-text-001-gemma3-adapter/experiment.yaml: add a reflection: block AND flip status: complete → status: closed.
   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: refuted
     surprises: |
       n=4 TIE at step 2000 turned into n=20 FAIL (30% win rate). Rule-based instructions made the adapter genuinely WORSE than prompting, not tied. Small-n evaluation was hiding a clear negative result.
     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |
       Instructions are not a cosmetic variable - they are first-order for this architecture. Noisy instructions force the prefix channel to compensate, destroying the style signal.
     next_experiment: 2026-04-text-002-gemma3-llm-instructions
     rolls_up_to_project: true
     rolls_up_to_program: true

7. UPDATE experiments/2026-04-text-002-gemma3-llm-instructions/experiment.yaml: add a reflection: block AND flip status: complete → status: closed.
   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: partially_confirmed
     surprises: |
       T3 style carryover stayed FAIL at n=20 despite T2 passing. The surface-feature metric cannot distinguish style match from noise; T3 is a broken instrument right now, not a real result. Also: delta between 001 and 002 was huge (40pp T2 swing) - larger than expected.
     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |
       C1 (adapter beats prompting) has first decisive evidence: 70 percent at n=20. T3 needs a better instrument (LLM-judge style-match) before it can guide future decisions. Architectural interventions (003 contrastive loss, encoder widening) are less urgent than fixing eval.
     next_experiment: none
     rolls_up_to_project: true

  "meta_commentary": "This is a tightly-scoped MVP finish: three new files (hf_push.py, close-experiment.sh), two existing bash scripts getting idempotent tag helpers + arg parsing, a PROCESS.md doc update, and two yaml backfills \u2014 all in one commit, no push, no tests beyond lint/dry-run.\n\nExecution gotchas:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 is a real trap.** The plan's Step 9.2 says `git rm -r experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke*`, but those dirs are untracked \u2014 their only content (`results/a.json`) is gitignored. Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.         Idea:
Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

6. UPDATE experiments/2026-04-text-001-gemma3-adapter/experiment.yaml: add a reflection: block AND flip status: complete → status: closed.
   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: refuted
     surprises: |
       n=4 TIE at step 2000 turned into n=20 FAIL (30% win rate). Rule-based instructions made the adapter genuinely WORSE than prompting, not tied. Small-n evaluation was hiding a clear negative result.
     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |
       Instructions are not a cosmetic variable - they are first-order for this architecture. Noisy instructions force the prefix channel to compensate, destroying the style signal.
     next_experiment: 2026-04-text-002-gemma3-llm-instructions

7. UPDATE experiments/2026-04-text-002-gemma3-llm-instructions/experiment.yaml: add a reflection: block AND flip status: complete → status: closed.

Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

)\n+\n+    revision = getattr(info, \"oid\", \"\")\n+    yaml_path.write_text(\n+        strip_checkpoint_block(yaml_text)\n+        + f\"checkpoint:\\n\"\n+        + f\"  hf_repo: {repo_id}\\n\"\n+ Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:

   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: refuted
     surprises: |
       n=4 TIE at step 2000 turned into n=20 FAIL (30% win rate). Rule-based instructions made the adapter genuinely WORSE than prompting, not tied. Small-n evaluation was hiding a clear negative result.

Gates in order (each dies on failure): (a) `experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml` exists; (b) `grep -qx 'status: finalized' experiment.yaml`; (c) `[[ $(wc -c < LEARNINGS.md | tr -d ' ') -ge 400 ]]`; (d) `! grep -q '^reflection:' experiment.yaml` (double-close prevention). Validate `hypothesis_outcome \u2208 {confirmed, partially_confirmed, refuted, unclear}` \u2014 die with allowed-list. Append `reflection:` block via a `python3 - <<'PY'` heredoc emitting YAML with `|`-indented literals for `surprises` and `corrections_to_prior_beliefs`. Flip status with `sed -i.bak 's/^status: finalized$/status: closed/' experiment.yaml && rm -f experiment.yaml.bak`. If `next_experiment != 'none'`: call `\"${REPO_ROOT}/tools/plan-experiment.sh\" \"$next_experiment\"` then `sed -i.bak \"s/^parent: null$/parent: ${experiment_id}/\" \"${REPO_ROOT}/experiments/${next_experiment}/experiment.yaml\"` and remove the bak. Roll-ups: derive `project_slug` via awk on `project:` line; if `rolls_up_to_project` truthy \u2192 `\"${EDITOR:-}\" \"${REPO_ROOT}/projects/${project_slug}/LEARNINGS.md\"` when EDITOR set, else echo `note: update projects/${project_slug}/LEARNINGS.md manually`; same pattern with `program/LEARNINGS.md` for `rolls_up_to_program`.   "meta_commentary": "This is a tightly-scoped MVP finish: three new files (hf_push.py, close-experiment.sh), two existing bash scripts getting idempotent tag helpers + arg parsing, a PROCESS.md doc update, and two yaml backfills \u2014 all in one commit, no push, no tests beyond lint/dry-run.\n\nExecution gotchas:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 is a real trap.** The plan's Step 9.2 says `git rm -r experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke*`, but those dirs are untracked \u2014 their only content (`results/a.json`) is gitignored. Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.         Idea:
Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.
   - Supports --dry-run that prints what would be pushed without pushing.
   - Update tools/finalize-experiment.sh to accept --push (invokes python tools/hf_push.py with the experiment id) and --checkpoint-path PATH (passed through).

5. UPDATE program/PROCESS.md:
   - Rename the existing 'Finalize' section to describe it as ROBOT WORK (mechanical capture).
   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). - [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: program/PROCESS.md\n@@\n-## Finalize\n+## Finalize (ROBOT WORK: mechanical capture)\n@@\n-Finalization requires a launch manifest, non-empty results, non-empty learnings, and non-`TBD` `headline` and `next` fields. It records result hashes and flips the experiment to the complete state used by the current toolchain.\n+Finalization requires a launch manifest, non-empty results, non-empty learnings, and non-`TBD` `headline` and `next` fields. It records result hashes and flips the experiment to the `finalized` state.\n+\n+Use `--push` to checkpoint the finalized experiment to Hugging Face, and `--checkpoint-path PATH` to include an external checkpoint file. The target namespace is `HF_NAMESPACE` or `peteromallet` by default. The token is read from `HF_TOKEN`, then `~/.cache/huggingface/token`. The push uploads `experiment.yaml`, `config.yaml`, `launch_manifest.json`, `requirements.lock`, `LEARNINGS.md`, `README.md`, `results/**`, and the optional checkpoint.\n \n Headline discipline matters: one sentence, actual result, no hedging placeholders, no `TBD`. If the result is negative, say so directly.\n \n+## Close (HUMAN WORK: learning capture)\n+\n+Close the experiment after finalization:\n+\n+```bash\n+tools/close-experiment.sh <id>\n+```\n+\n+Closing requires `status: finalized`, blocks boilerplate by requiring `LEARNINGS.md` to be at least 400 bytes, and records the human reflection fields: `hypothesis_outcome`, `surprises`, `corrections_to_prior_beliefs`, `next_experiment`, `rolls_up_to_project`, and `rolls_up_to_program`.\n+\n+If `next_experiment` is not `none`, the close script creates the next experiment shell with the current experiment as parent. If roll-up flags are true, it opens `projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md` and/or `program/LEARNINGS.md` in `$EDITOR`; without `$EDITOR`, it prints a manual-update note.\n+\n ## Write up\n@@\n - Zero `TBD` at finalize.\n - No manifest means no experiment.\n+- An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.\n@@\n - no manifest = no experiment.\n - n=4 is a smoke test, not an eval.\n+- Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.\n*** End Patch\n"

        Idea:
Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

      "description": "Create `tools/hf_push.py` (~70\u2013120 lines, executable). Structure: shebang `#!/usr/bin/env python3`; argparse with positional `experiment_id`, optional `--checkpoint-path PATH`, `--dry-run`. Resolve `repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]` and `exp_dir = repo_root/\"experiments\"/experiment_id`; exit 1 if missing. Parse `project:` and `headline:` from `experiment.yaml` via regex (mirroring finalize-experiment.sh's awk-based `yaml_value` \u2014 do NOT import PyYAML). Compute `repo_id = f\"{os.environ.get('HF_NAMESPACE','peteromallet')}/prompt-adapters-{project_slug}\"`, `branch = f\"exp-{experiment_id}\"`. Token resolution: `HF_TOKEN` env \u2192 `Path('~/.cache/huggingface/token').expanduser()` \u2192 None (dry-run tolerates missing; live errors loudly). Build ordered upload list of EXISTING files only: `experiment.yaml`, `config.yaml`, `launch_manifest.json`, `requirements.lock`, `LEARNINGS.md`, `README.md`, recursive walk of `results/` excluding `.gitkeep`; append `--checkpoint-path` file if given. Generate HF-side `README.md` in a tempdir containing project link, experiment id, headline text, and back-link to the GitHub repo derived from `git config --get remote.origin.url` (fallback `https://github.com/peteromallet/prompt-adapters`). `--dry-run` branch: print repo_id, branch, sorted upload list to stdout and exit 0 \u2014 MUST NOT `import huggingface_hub` before this branch (lazy import inside live-path block only). Live path: `from huggingface_hub import HfApi`; `api=HfApi(token=token)`; `create_repo(repo_type=\"model\", exist_ok=True)`; `create_branch(exist_ok=True)`; stage all files into one tempdir preserving exp-relative paths; single `upload_folder` call; capture `CommitInfo.oid`. On live success, rewrite `experiment.yaml` with idempotent `checkpoint:` block (strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block through the next top-level key or EOF, then append fresh block containing `hf_repo`, `hf_branch`, `hf_revision_sha`). `chmod +x` the file.",

      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Create `tools/close-experiment.sh` (~120\u2013180 lines, executable). Shebang + `set -euo pipefail` + `usage`/`die` helpers. Positional `<experiment_id>`; optional `--hypothesis-outcome V`, `--surprises V`, `--corrections V`, `--next-experiment V`, `--rolls-up-project true|false`, `--rolls-up-program true|false`. Any unset value \u2192 interactive `read -r -p` prompt (bools as `[y/N]`). Gates in order (each dies on failure): (a) `experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml` exists; (b) `grep -qx 'status: finalized' experiment.yaml`; (c) `[[ $(wc -c < LEARNINGS.md | tr -d ' ') -ge 400 ]]`; (d) `! grep -q '^reflection:' experiment.yaml` (double-close prevention). Validate `hypothesis_outcome \u2208 {confirmed, partially_confirmed, refuted, unclear}` \u2014 die with allowed-list. Append `reflection:` block via a `python3 - <<'PY'` heredoc emitting YAML with `|`-indented literals for `surprises` and `corrections_to_prior_beliefs`. Flip status with `sed -i.bak 's/^status: finalized$/status: closed/' experiment.yaml && rm -f experiment.yaml.bak`. If `next_experiment != 'none'`: call `\"${REPO_ROOT}/tools/plan-experiment.sh\" \"$next_experiment\"` then `sed -i.bak \"s/^parent: null$/parent: ${experiment_id}/\" \"${REPO_ROOT}/experiments/${next_experiment}/experiment.yaml\"` and remove the bak. Roll-ups: derive `project_slug` via awk on `project:` line; if `rolls_up_to_project` truthy \u2192 `\"${EDITOR:-}\" \"${REPO_ROOT}/projects/${project_slug}/LEARNINGS.md\"` when EDITOR set, else echo `note: update projects/${project_slug}/LEARNINGS.md manually`; same pattern with `program/LEARNINGS.md` for `rolls_up_to_program`. Create idempotent annotated tag `exp-<id>-closed` using the SAME inlined helper from T2 (NOT sourced). Final echo `closed experiments/<id>`. `chmod +x`.",

  "meta_commentary": "This is a tightly-scoped MVP finish: three new files (hf_push.py, close-experiment.sh), two existing bash scripts getting idempotent tag helpers + arg parsing, a PROCESS.md doc update, and two yaml backfills \u2014 all in one commit, no push, no tests beyond lint/dry-run.\n\nExecution gotchas:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 is a real trap.** The plan's Step 9.2 says `git rm -r experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke*`, but those dirs are untracked \u2014 their only content (`results/a.json`) is gitignored. Executing that command literally will fail with a pathspec error and abort the commit. T1 uses plain `rm -rf`; T8 explicitly notes not to `git rm` these paths. Follow T1/T8 wording, not the plan's Step 9.2.\n\n2. **Tag helper must be inlined three times.** Do not refactor into a shared sourced file even though it looks like duplication. The brief says 'no fancy features' and the plan's refinement point 4 explicitly calls this out. Three near-identical ~10-line helpers in launch/finalize/close scripts is correct.\n\n3. **Lazy import of huggingface_hub is load-bearing.** `--dry-run` is part of the validation suite and the dev machine may not have `huggingface_hub` installed. If you place `import huggingface_hub` at the top of hf_push.py, validation step 3 will fail with ModuleNotFoundError. Put it inside the live-path branch. Verify with `grep -n huggingface_hub tools/hf_push.py` \u2014 the line number should be well past any dry-run code path.\n\n4. **Status terminology transition.** Existing finalize-experiment.sh writes `status: complete`. The plan renames this to `status: finalized` going forward (T3). Simultaneously, T7 backfills 001 and 002 directly from `complete` \u2192 `closed` (they skip the `finalized` intermediate state \u2014 they are a one-off migration, NOT run through close-experiment.sh). Do not run close-experiment.sh on 001 or 002; it would fail the status gate anyway since they are already `complete`, not `finalized`.\n\n5. **`tools/new-experiment.sh` is a typo in the brief.** Only `tools/plan-experiment.sh` exists. close-experiment.sh calls plan-experiment.sh and then uses sed to set `parent:` on the generated experiment.yaml (plan-experiment.sh has no parent arg).\n\n6. **Idempotent checkpoint-block rewrite.** In hf_push.py's live path, the yaml write-back must strip any existing `^checkpoint:` block (through the next top-level key or EOF) before appending the fresh block. This makes repeated live pushes safe \u2014 one block, not N blocks.\n\n7. **Do NOT retroactively tag 001 and 002.** They predate the tagging system. Only future launch/finalize/close runs create tags. This is explicit in the plan's Step 7.3.\n\n8. **Scope discipline.** Do NOT touch text-ip-adapter/. Do NOT add tests beyond the dry-run and syntax lints. Do NOT add PyYAML or any other dep. Do NOT refactor adjacent code that happens to be in the same files.\n\n9. **Final commit must be single.** All seven other tasks stage changes but do not commit. T8 is the only commit point. The exact message is `Add close-experiment step and HF checkpoint push; clean up smoke-test artifacts` \u2014 no trailing period, no coauthor line needed per the plan.\n\nDebt watch items are unrelated to this plan (they reference reigh-app/travel-between-images, wan2gp, worker orchestrator) \u2014 they come from other plans in the registry and can be ignored for this task.\n\nThe `tasks` array is sized to the work: 8 tasks for 9 plan steps (Steps 8 and 9 are merged into T8 since validation and commit are tightly coupled). No 'inspect' tasks \u2014 executor reads files as part of editing them.",

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.         Idea:
Finish the reproducibility MVP that megaplan build-the-mvp-reproducibility-20260424-1941 left incomplete. Specifically:

1. DELETE the three test-artifact experiment dirs leftover from plan-experiment.sh smoke tests:
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-empty/
   - experiments/9999-99-test-999-smoke-headline/

2. ADD git tagging to the existing scripts:
   - tools/launch-experiment.sh: after writing launch_manifest.json, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-launch' pointing at HEAD, with a short message referencing the experiment id. Idempotent: if the tag already exists at the same sha, skip without error.
   - tools/finalize-experiment.sh: after populating results block, create an annotated git tag 'exp-<id>-finalized' pointing at HEAD. Same idempotency rule.

3. WRITE tools/close-experiment.sh (~120-180 lines of bash). This is the post-finalize human-reflection step that adds a distinct 'closed' lifecycle state.
   - Takes one arg: experiment id.
   - Refuse if status != 'finalized'.
   - Refuse if experiments/<id>/LEARNINGS.md is smaller than 400 bytes (boilerplate check).
   - Support interactive prompts OR non-interactive flags (--hypothesis-outcome, --surprises, --corrections, --next-experiment, --rolls-up-project, --rolls-up-program).
   - Write answers into experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml under a new top-level 'reflection:' block with subfields: hypothesis_outcome (confirmed|partially_confirmed|refuted|unclear), surprises (multi-line string), corrections_to_prior_beliefs (multi-line string), next_experiment (id or 'none'), rolls_up_to_project (bool), rolls_up_to_program (bool).
   - If next_experiment is an id (not 'none'), run tools/new-experiment.sh for that id with parent set to current.
   - If rolls_up_to_project: open projects/<slug>/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - If rolls_up_to_program: open program/LEARNINGS.md in $EDITOR if set, else print a note.
   - Git tag 'exp-<id>-closed' annotated.
   - Flip experiment.yaml 'status: finalized' → 'status: closed'.

4. WRITE tools/hf_push.py (~70-120 lines of python). Called from finalize-experiment.sh when user passes --push flag.
   - Reads experiments/<id>/experiment.yaml for project slug.
   - HF repo: ${HF_NAMESPACE:-peteromallet}/prompt-adapters-${project_slug}. Model-type repo.
   - HF branch: exp-${experiment_id}.
   - Reads HF_TOKEN from env or ~/.cache/huggingface/token.
   - Uses huggingface_hub Python SDK: HfApi.create_repo (exist_ok=True), HfApi.create_branch (exist_ok=True), HfApi.upload_folder or upload_file.
   - Uploads: experiment.yaml, config.yaml, launch_manifest.json, requirements.lock, LEARNINGS.md, README.md, everything under results/, plus the checkpoint if a --checkpoint-path arg is given (checkpoint file likely lives outside the repo dir).
   - Also auto-generates an HF-side README.md with project link, experiment id, headline result from experiment.yaml, and a link back to the GitHub repo path.
   - On success, writes the HF repo + branch + commit sha back into experiment.yaml as 'checkpoint.hf_repo', 'checkpoint.hf_branch', 'checkpoint.hf_revision_sha'.

   - Add a new 'Close' section after Finalize that describes HUMAN WORK: hypothesis_outcome, surprises, corrections_to_prior_beliefs, next_experiment, rolls_up decisions. Reference tools/close-experiment.sh.
   - Add HF push documentation under Finalize: the --push flag, HF_NAMESPACE env var, what gets uploaded.
   - Add a 'Hard rule' to the rules list: 'An experiment is not an experiment until it is CLOSED. Finalized means data captured; closed means learning captured.'
   - Add an 'Anti-pattern': 'Treating finalized as good enough — this accumulates results without reflection.'

   reflection:
     hypothesis_outcome: refuted
     surprises: |
       n=4 TIE at step 2000 turned into n=20 FAIL (30% win rate). Rule-based instructions made the adapter genuinely WORSE than prompting, not tied. Small-n evaluation was hiding a clear negative result.
     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |
       Instructions are not a cosmetic variable - they are first-order for this architecture. Noisy instructions force the prefix channel to compensate, destroying the style signal.
     next_experiment: 2026-04-text-002-gemma3-llm-instructions
     rolls_up_to_project: true
     rolls_up_to_program: true

   reflection:

     corrections_to_prior_beliefs: |
       C1 (adapter beats prompting) has first decisive evidence: 70 percent at n=20. T3 needs a better instrument (LLM-judge style-match) before it can guide future decisions. Architectural interventions (003 contrastive loss, encoder widening) are less urgent than fixing eval.
     next_experiment: none
     rolls_up_to_project: true
